4.
Flow categories, the cube flow category, and signed cubical realizations

We give a brief summary of the cube flow category, introduced by Lawson, Lipshitz, Sarkar
[
LLS20
]
.

4.1.
The cube category and sign assignments.

Definition 4.1

(
[
LLS20
]
)
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
n>0
, we define the cube category
\underline{2}^{n}
as follows:

•

\Ob(\underline{2}^{n})=2^{\{1,\ldots,n\}}
, that is, subsets of
\{1,\ldots,n\}
.

•

For two objects
u,v\in\underline{2}^{n}
,
\text{Hom}(u,v)
is empty unless
u\supseteq v
, in which case there is a single morphism
\phi_{u,v}:u\to v
.

We often use the notation
u<v
as shorthand for
u\subset v
(and
u\leq v
as shorthand for
u\subseteq v
). Thre is a grading on the objects of
\underline{2}^{n}
given by
\gr(u)=|u|
. If
u>v
and
\gr(u)=\gr(v)+i
, we use the notation
u>_{i}v
. In the case where
i=1
(
i=2
), we call
\phi_{u,v}
an
edge
(a
face
). If we want to emphasize that
u,v
are subsets of
\{1,\ldots,n\}
, we often use labels
S,T
.

Definition 4.2
.

A
sign assignment

\widetilde{s}
is a function from the edges
u>_{1}v
of the cube category
\underline{2}^{n}
to
\mathbb{F}_{2}
such that, for any face
u>_{2}w
with intermediate vertices
v_{1},v_{2}
, we have
\widetilde{s}_{u,v_{1}}+\widetilde{s}_{u,v_{2}}+\widetilde{s}_{v_{1},w}+\widetilde{s}_{v_{2},w}=1
. (We abbreviate
\widetilde{s}_{u,v}:=\widetilde{s}(\phi_{u,v})
).

The
standard sign assignment

s
is the sign assignment defined as follows: For an edge
T>_{1}S
,
T\backslash S=\{j\}
, we define

s_{T,S}:=\#\{i\in S:i<j\}\pmod{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}.

When viewing a sign assignment
\tilde{s}
as a cochain in
C^{1}_{\cell}([0,1]^{n},\mathbb{F}_{2})
,
\delta s\in C^{2}_{\cell}([0,1]^{n},\mathbb{F}_{2})
is the constant map that maps all faces to
1
. Note that any sign assignment
\tilde{s}
differs from
s
by a cocycle
\delta c\in C^{2}_{\cell}([0,1]^{n};\mathbb{F}_{2})
.

Definition 4.3
.

The
index assingment

s_{\mathbb{Z}}
is defined as follows: For an edge
T>_{1}S
,
T\backslash S=\{j\}
, we define
s_{\mathbb{Z}}(T,S):=\#\{i\in S:i<j\}\in\mathbb{Z}
.

4.2.
Manifolds with corners and
\langle n\rangle
-manifolds.

Definition 4.4
.

A
facet
of
X
is the closure of a codimension-
1
boundary-component of
X
. A
multifacet
of
X
is a (possibly empty) union of disjoint facets of
X
. A manifold with corners
X
is a
multifaced manifold
if every
x\in X
belongs to exactly
c(x)
facets of
X
. We define an
\langle n\rangle
-manifold to be a multifaceted manifold
X
with an ordered n-tuple
(\partial_{1}X,\ldots,\partial_{n}X)
of multifacets
X
satisfying

•

\bigcup_{i}\partial_{i}X=\partial X
.

•

For all distinct
i,j
,
\partial_{i}X\cap\partial_{j}X
is a multifacet of both
\partial_{i}X
and
\partial_{j}X
.

Lemma 4.5
.

The space
\Pi^{n-1}
can be viewed as an
\langle n-1\rangle
-manifold by defining

\partial_{i}\Pi^{n-1}=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\\
|S|=i\end{subarray}}F_{S}

for
1\leq i\leq n-1
.

Proof.

The proof is due to
[
LLS20
]

We check the following:

(1)

Each
\partial_{i}\Pi^{n-1}
is a multifacet

(2)

Every point
x
belongs to
c(x)
facets

(3)

\bigcup_{i}\partial_{i}\Pi^{n-1}=\partial\Pi^{n-1}

(4)

For each
i\neq j
,
\partial_{i}\Pi^{n-1}\cap\partial_{j}\Pi^{n-1}
is a multifacet of
\partial_{i}\Pi^{n-1}
(and
\partial_{j}\Pi^{n-1}
).

To prove
(1)
, we use Lemma
3.5
: if
F_{S}\cap F_{T}\neq\emptyset
, then either
S\subseteq T
or
T\subseteq S
. Therefore,
\partial_{i}\Pi^{n-1}
is indeed a
disjoint
union of the faces
S
, where
|S|=i
.

To prove
(2)
, fix a point
x\in\Pi^{n-1}
and note that
x
is possibly contained in facets, which are of the form
F_{S}
. For convenience, we call these facets
F_{S_{1}},\ldots,F_{S_{m}}
, where
S_{1}<\ldots<S_{m}
(the collection of
S_{i}
is possibly empty). From the construction
\Pi^{n-1}=\bigcap_{\emptyset\neq S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}}H_{S}
, where
H_{S}
are half-spaces, we see that
x
is contained in a coordinate neighborhood diffeomorphic to
\bigcap_{1\leq i\leq m}H_{S_{i}}\cong\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}:y_{1}\geq 0,\ldots,y_{m}\geq 0\}
.

Point
(3)
is immediate from the definitions.

For part
(4)
, let
F_{S}
be a facet of
\partial_{i}\Pi^{n-1}
. it suffices to show that
F_{S}\cap\partial_{j}\Pi^{n-1}
is a multifacet of
F_{S}
. We simply need to show that
F_{S}\cap F_{T}
is either empty or a facet of
F_{S}
. But Lemma
3.5
says that
F_{S}\cap F_{T}
is empty unless
T\subset S
or
S\subset T
. The map
f_{S}:F_{S}\to\Pi^{i-1}\times\Pi^{k-i-1}
of Lemma
3.4
identifies
F_{S}\cap F_{T}
with

\begin{cases}F_{T}^{\prime}\times\Pi^{k-i-1}&if $T\subset S$\\
\Pi^{i-1}\times F_{T}^{\prime}&if $S\subset T$\end{cases},

where if
T\subset S
, then
F_{T}^{\prime}\cong\Pi^{j-1}\times\Pi^{i-j-1}
is a facet of
\Pi^{i-1}
and if
S\subset T
, then
F_{T}^{\prime}\cong\Pi^{j-i-1}\times\Pi^{k-j-1}
is a facet of
\Pi^{k-i-1}
.
∎

4.3.
Signed flow categories

We review some notation about flow categories from Cohen-Jones-Segal
[
CJS95
]
.

Definition 4.6

(
[
CJS95
]
)
.

A
flow category

\mathscr{C}
is a topological category such that the objects
\Ob(\mathscr{C})
form a discrete space, and the morphisms satisfy the following:

(FC-1)

For any
x\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
,
\text{Hom}(x,x)=\{\text{Id}\}
; The identity morphisms in a flow category are a somewhat special case and it is often convenient to disregard them. We define the
moduli space

\mathcal{M}(x,y)
from
x
to
y
to be
\text{Hom}(x,y)
if
x\neq y
and empty if
x=y
.

(FC-2)

For any
x,y\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
with
\gr(x)-\gr(y)=k
,
\mathcal{M}(x,y)
is a (possibly empty) compact (
k-1
)-dimensional
\langle k-1\rangle
-manifold; and

(FC-3)

The composition maps combine to form a diffeomorphism of
\langle k-2\rangle
-manifolds

(7)

\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})\backslash\{x,z\}\\
\gr(y)-\gr(x)=i\end{subarray}}\mathcal{M}(y,z)\times\mathcal{M}(x,y)\cong\partial_{i}\mathcal{M}(x,z).

For any flow category
\mathscr{C}
, define
\Sigma^{k}\mathscr{C}
to be the flow category obtained by increasing the gradings of
\mathscr{C}
up by
k
.

Definition 4.7
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a flow category. For
x,y\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
, we define
\widehat{A}_{x,y}
to be the (possibly empty) set of path components of
\mathcal{M}(x,y)
. The composition maps in (
7
) descend to composition maps
\widehat{A}_{y,z}\times\widehat{A}_{x,y}\to\widehat{A}_{x,z}
, which we denote by
(\gamma,\xi)\mapsto\gamma\circ\xi
.

We generalize the notion of a flow category slightly by introducing (
\mathbb{F}_{2}
-valued) signs on components of the moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}(x,y)
:

Definition 4.8
.

A
signed flow category

\mathscr{C}
is a flow category
\mathscr{C}
equipped with a sign map
\sigma:\coprod_{x,y\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})}\widehat{A}_{x,y}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}
such that
\sigma(\gamma\circ\xi)=\sigma(\gamma)+\sigma(\xi)
for all
\gamma\in\widehat{A}_{y,z}
,
\xi\in\widehat{A}_{x,y}
. We call
\sigma
a
sign map
. Every (unsigned) flow category
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
comes with a trivial sign map
\sigma_{0}
: simply define
\sigma_{0}(\gamma)=0
for all
\gamma\in\widehat{A}_{x,y}
,
x,y\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
.

We often say
unsigned flow category
when referring to a flow category to avoid any confusion.

Notation 4.9
.

In the case that
\gr(y)=\gr(x)+1
and
p\in\mathcal{M}(y,x)
, we use the notation
\sigma(p):=\sigma(\{p\})
.

Definition 4.10
.

Given a (signed or unsigned) flow category
\mathscr{C}
, we can define a cochain complex with
\mathbb{F}_{2}
coefficients
C_{\mathcal{M}}^{*}(\mathscr{C};\mathbb{F}_{2})
as follows:

(K-1)

The set of generators is
\Ob(\mathscr{C})
, with a generator
x
having cohomological grading
\gr(x)
.

(K-2)

For
\gr(y)=\gr(x)+1
, the coefficient of
y
in
\delta x
is
\#(\mathcal{M}(y,x))
.

4.4.
The cube flow category

We introduce the cube flow category, which records the moduli space of “flowlines” between vertices in the cube.

Definition 4.11
.

Fix an integer
n>0
, The objects of the cube flow category
\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)
are the same as the objects of the cube category
\underline{2}^{n}
, that is, subsets of
\{1,\ldots,n\}
. The grading on the objects is the same as the grading in
\underline{2}^{n}
and the partial ordering
\geq
is also inherited from
\underline{2}^{n}
.

The space
\mathcal{M}(u,v)
is defined to be empty unless
u>v
. In the case
u>v
and
|u|-|v|=k>0
, we define
\mathcal{M}(u,v)=\Pi^{k-1}
, the
(k-1)
-dimensional permutohedron. The composition map
\mathcal{M}(v,w)\times\mathcal{M}(u,v)
is defined as follows: Assume
u>v>w
and
|u|-|v|=k
,
|v|-|w|=l
Let
u\backslash w=\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{k+l}\}
, where
a_{1}<\ldots<a_{k+l}
. Let
S
be the set of
s\in\{1,\ldots,k+l\}
satisfying
a_{s}\in v
. By Lemma
3.4
, there is a corresponding facet
F_{S}\subset\Pi^{k+l-1}=\mathcal{M}(u,w)
, and we define the composition by

\mathcal{M}(v,w)\times\mathcal{M}(u,v)\xhookrightarrow{f_{S}^{-1}}F_{S}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}(u,w).

Lemma 4.12
.

Definition
4.11
defines a flow category.

Proof.

See
[
LLS20
]
, Lemma 3.17.
∎

4.5.
Signed cubical flow categories

Definition 4.13

(
[
LLS20
]
, Definition 3.21)
.

A
cubical flow category
is a flow category
\mathscr{C}
equipped with a grading-preserving functor
\mathfrak{f}:\Sigma^{k}\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)
for some
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
so that for each
x,y\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
,
\mathfrak{f}:\mathcal{M}(x,y)\to\mathcal{M}(\mathfrak{f}(x),\mathfrak{f}(y))
is a (trivial) covering map.

Note that if
(\mathscr{C},\mathfrak{f})
is a cubical flow category, then
\mathcal{M}(x,y)
can only be nonempty (
x>y
) if
\mathfrak{f}(x)>\mathfrak{f}(y)
, and in this case, must be a (possibly empty) disjoint union of permutohedra.

Definition 4.14
.

A
signed cubical flow category

(\mathscr{C},\mathfrak{f},\sigma)
is a cubical flow category
(\mathscr{C},\mathfrak{f})
equipped with the additional structure of a signed flow category, that is, a sign map
\sigma
.

The cube category
\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)
is not a signed cubical flow category, so there should be no expectation that the sign map
\sigma
satisfies any sort of “compatibility” condition with respect to the covering map
\mathfrak{f}:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)
.

Example 4.14.1
.

The Khovanov flow category
\mathscr{C}_{K}(L)
(
[
LS14a
]
, Definition 5.3) associates to a link diagram
L
with
n
crossings a cubical flow category
\mathscr{C}_{K}(L)
. For any
v\in\underline{2}^{n}
, the subset of
\Ob(\mathscr{C}_{K}(L))
that maps to
v
are precisely the Khovanov generators
x
that lie over the
v
-resolution of
L
.

For any
u,v\in\underline{2}^{n}
with
|u|-|v|=1
, and any
x\in\mathfrak{f}^{-1}(u)=F(u)
,
y\in\mathfrak{f}^{-1}(v)=F(v)
, the moduli space is

\mathscr{C}_{K}(L)(x,y)=\begin{cases}\{\text{pt}_{x,y}\}&if $x$ appears in $\delta_{Kh}(y)$\\
\emptyset&otherwise.\\
\end{cases}

The
1
-dimensional moduli spaces takes some work to construct, and the higher-dimensional spaces are defined inductively. The
\mathbb{F}_{2}
-coefficient Khovanov chain complex
KC^{*}(L;\mathbb{F}_{2})
is canonically identified with
C_{\mathcal{M}}^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{K}(L);\mathbb{F}_{2})
.

Example 4.14.2
.

The odd Khovanov flow category
\mathscr{C}_{K,o}(L)
is a signed flow category, which as a flow category, equals
\mathscr{C}_{K}(L)
precisely. Now
\mathscr{C}_{K,o}(L)
has a sign map
\sigma
which arises from the odd Khovanov functor
\mathfrak{F}_{o}:(\underline{2}^{n})^{\text{op}}\to\mathbb{Z}\text{--Mod}
in
[
SSS20
]
, Section 5.1. Namely, for each edge
u>_{1}v
in
\underline{2}^{n}
and Khovanov generator
y
with
\mathfrak{f}(y)=v
, we write

\mathfrak{F}_{o}(\phi^{\text{op}}_{v,u})(y)=\sum_{x\in F_{o}(u)}\epsilon_{x,y}x,

where by definition of
\mathfrak{F}_{o}
,
\epsilon_{x,y}\in\{-1,0,1\}
for each
x\in F_{o}(u)
, and more specifically,
\epsilon_{x,y}\in\{-1,1\}
for each
x\in F_{o}(u)
with
\mathscr{C}_{K,o}(L)(x,y)\neq\emptyset
. We impose the identity
\epsilon_{x,y}=(-1)^{\sigma_{x,y}(\{\text{pt}_{x,y}\})}
, which defines
\sigma_{x,y}
for
\gr(x)-\gr(y)=1
. For sequences
x_{m},\ldots x_{0}
such that
\gr(x_{i})-\gr(x_{i-1})=1
,
\mathcal{M}(x_{i},x_{i-1})=\{\text{pt}_{x_{i},x_{i-1}}\}
, we define

\sigma(\{\text{pt}_{x_{m},x_{m-1}}\}\circ\ldots\circ\{\text{pt}_{x_{1},x_{0}}\}):=\sigma(\{\text{pt}_{x_{m},x_{m-1}}\})+\ldots+\sigma(\{\text{pt}_{x_{1},x_{0}}\}).

This defnition allows us to fully extend
\sigma
to a sign map on
\mathscr{C}_{K,o}(L)
.

Definition 4.15
.

There is also the notion of a sign assignment on a cubical flow category
\mathscr{C}
. A
cubical sign assignment

\widetilde{S}
is a function

\widetilde{S}:\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\\
\gr(y)=\gr(x)+1\end{subarray}}\mathcal{M}(y,x)\to\mathbb{F}_{2}

from the set of
0
-dimensional moduli spaces to
\mathbb{F}_{2}
such that if
I
is an interval in a
2
D moduli space
\mathcal{M}(z,x)
and
\partial I=\{p\circ q,p^{\prime}\circ q^{\prime}\}
, then

(8)

\widetilde{S}(p)+\widetilde{S}(q)+\widetilde{S}(p^{\prime})+\widetilde{S}(q^{\prime})=1.

Note how this notion of a sign assignment is a generalization of the sign assignment on the cube category (see Definition
4.2
). Indeed, if we identify edges
v>_{1}u
of
\underline{2}^{n}
with the corresponding (unique) morphisms
p\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,u)
, the criterion
\widetilde{s}_{u,v_{1}}+\widetilde{s}_{u,v_{2}}+\widetilde{s}_{v_{1},w}+\widetilde{s}_{v_{2},w}=1
of Definition
4.2
is equivalent to the criterion (
8
) for the cube flow category.

Definition 4.16
.

Let
\mathfrak{f}:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)
be a signed cubical flow category. We define the
standard cubical sign assignment

S
as the pullback of the standard sign assignment
s
along
\mathfrak{f}
, plus the sign map
\sigma
. Namely, if
p\in\mathfrak{f}^{-1}(\mathcal{M}(v,u))
for
v>_{1}u
, we define
S(p):=s(v,u)+\sigma(p)
. If
\mathscr{C}
is an unsigned cubical flow category, we simply omit the
\sigma
-component, defining
S(p):=s(v,u)
. Similarly, we define the
cubical index assignment
by
S_{\mathbb{Z}}(p):=s_{\mathbb{Z}}(v,u)
.

In practice, we simply say
standard sign assignment
and
index assignment
if the context is clear.

Example 4.16.1
.

Given a cubical flow category
\mathfrak{f}:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)
with sign map
\sigma
, we define a cochain complex with
\mathbb{Z}
coefficients
C_{\mathcal{M}}^{*}(\mathscr{C};\mathbb{Z})
as follows:

(K’-1)

The set of generators is
\Ob(\mathscr{C})
, with a generator
x
having cohomological grading
\gr(x)
.

(K’-2)

The differential is defined on generators
x
by

(9)

dx=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in\mathcal{M}(y,x)\\
\gr(y)=\gr(x)+1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{S(p)}y,

where
S
is the standard cubical sign assignment defined in Example
4.16
.

Remark 4.16.1
.

Note that given the odd Khovanov flow category
\mathscr{C}_{K,o}(L)
from Example
4.14.2
, the cochain complex
C_{\mathcal{M}}^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{K,o}(L);\mathbb{Z})
recovers the odd Khovanov chain complex
Kc_{o}^{*}(L)
(see
[
ORS13
]
). If we use the (unsigned) Khovanov flow category
\mathscr{C}_{K}(L)
, the cochain complex
C_{\mathcal{M}}^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{K}(L);\mathbb{Z})
recovers the even Khovanov chain complex.

4.6.
Signed cubical flow categories are functors from the cube to the signed Burnside category

The construction of the Odd Khovanov homotopy type in
[
SSS20
]
does not rely on cubical flow categories, but instead unitary, lax functors
\underline{2}^{n}\to\mathscr{B}_{\sigma}
from the cube category to the signed Burnside category. We want to show that these objects are equivalent in study, since we want to show that our definition of the odd Khovanov homotopy type is equivalent to the construction in
[
SSS20
]
. The following constructions that we define follow
[
LLS20
]
, Section 4.3, with the additional detail of adding sign maps:

Construction 4.17
.

Fix a signed cubical flow category
\mathfrak{f}:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)
with a sign map
\sigma:\coprod_{x,y}\widehat{A}_{x,y}\to\mathbb{F}_{2}
. We construct a unitary, lax
2
-functor
F:\underline{2}^{n}\to\mathscr{B}_{\sigma}
from the cube to the signed Burnside category. Namely, we need to define the sets
X_{u}:=F(u)
, the correspondences
(A_{u,w},s_{A_{u,v}},t_{A_{u,v}},\sigma_{A_{u,v}}):=F(\phi_{u,v})
, and the isomorphisms of correspondences
F_{u,v,w}:A_{v,w}\times_{X_{v}}A_{u,v}\to A_{u,w}
.

•

For
u\in\underline{2}^{n}
, define
X_{u}:=\mathfrak{f}^{-1}(u)
.

•

For
u,v\in\underline{2}^{n}
,
u>v
, define
A_{u,v}:=\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in X_{u}\\
y\in X_{v}\end{subarray}}\widehat{A}_{x,y}
.
s_{A_{u,v}}
and
t_{A_{u,v}}
, when restricted to the subset
\widehat{A}_{x,y}
, are simply constant maps to
x
and
y
respectively. Similarly,
\sigma_{A_{u,v}}:=(-1)^{\sigma}
.

•

For
\gamma\in\widehat{A}_{y,z}\subseteq A_{v,w}
,
\xi\in\widehat{A}_{x,y}\subseteq A_{u,v}
, define
F_{u,v,w}(\gamma,\xi)=\gamma\circ\xi
.

Construction 4.18
.

Fix a strictly unitary lax
2
-functor
\underline{2}^{n}\to\mathscr{B}_{\sigma}
. We build a signed cubical flow category
\mathfrak{f}:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)
with a sign map
\sigma
.

•

\Ob(\mathscr{C})=\coprod_{u\in\underline{2}^{n}}F(u)
. For
x\in F(u)
, we define
\mathfrak{f}(x)=u
.

•

Let
x,y\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
, with
\mathfrak{f}(x)=u
,
\mathfrak{f}(y)=v
. Consider the subset
\widehat{B}_{x,y}=s^{-1}(x)\cap t^{-1}(y)\subseteq A_{u,v}=F(\phi_{u,v})
. For
x,y\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
, we define

\mathcal{M}(x,y)=\widehat{B}_{x,y}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,v).

As usual,
\widehat{A}_{x,y}
denotes the set of components of
\mathcal{M}(x,y)
.

•

For a component
\gamma=\{p\}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,v)\in\widehat{A}_{x,y}
, define
\sigma(\gamma)
implicitly by
(-1)^{\sigma(\gamma)}=\sigma_{A_{u,v}}(p)
.

•

For
\mathfrak{f}(x)=u
,
\mathfrak{f}(y)=v
,
\mathfrak{f}(z)=w
, we define the composition map

\circ:\left(\widehat{B}_{y,z}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,w)\right)\times\left(\widehat{B}_{x,y}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,v)\right)\to\widehat{B}_{x,z}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,w)

to be
F_{u,v,w}
on the
\widehat{B}
factors and
\circ_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}
on the
\mathcal{M}
factors.

Lemma 4.19
.

Construction
4.17
defines a unitary, lax
2
-functor
F:\underline{2}^{n}\to\mathscr{B}_{\sigma}
. Similarly, Construction
4.18
defines a signed flow category
\mathfrak{f}:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)
with a sign map
\sigma
.

Proof.

We certainly have from
[
LLS20
]
, Lemma 4.18 that forgetting the sign maps
\sigma_{A_{u,v}}
,
F
is a unitary, lax
2
-functor
\underline{2}^{n}\to\mathscr{B}
. The compatibility of the sign maps
\sigma_{A_{u,v}}
under composition follows from the naturality of the sign map
\sigma
.

We also have from
[
LLS20
]
, Lemma 4.20 that Construction
4.18
defines a flow category. And similarly, the compatibility of
\sigma
under composition follows from the naturality of the sign maps
\sigma_{A_{u,v}}
.
∎

4.7.
Cubical neat embeddings

Fix a cube flow category
\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)
and fix a tuple
\mathbf{d}=(d_{0},\ldots,d_{n-1})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
and a real number
R>0
(we think of
R
as a “large” number). For any
u>v
in
\Ob(\mathscr{C}_{C}(n))=\underline{2}^{n}
, define

E_{u,v}=\left[\prod_{i=|v|}^{|u|-1}(-R,R)^{d_{i}}\right]\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,v).

We can think of
E_{u,v}
as a “thickened” moduli space. For
u>v>w
in
\Ob(\mathscr{C}_{C}(n))
, there is a map
E_{v,w}\times E_{u,v}\to E_{u,w}
given by:

\displaystyle E_{v,w}\times E_{u,v}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=|w|}^{|v|-1}(-R,R)^{d_{i}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,w)\times\prod_{i=|v|}^{|u|-1}(-R,R)^{d_{i}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,v)

\displaystyle\cong\prod_{i=|w|}^{|u|-1}(-R,R)^{d_{i}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,w)\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,v)

\displaystyle\xhookrightarrow{\text{Id}\times\circ}\prod_{i=|w|}^{|u|-1}(-R,R)^{d_{i}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,w).

Now we discuss cubical neat embeddings: a way to fit the moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}(x,y)
into our thickened moduli spaces
E_{u,v}
.

Definition 4.20
.

A
cubical neat embedding

\iota
of a (signed or unsigned) flow category
\mathscr{C}
relative to a tuple
\mathbf{d}=(d_{0},\ldots,d_{n-1})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
consists of neat embeddings
\iota_{x,y}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,y)\to E_{\mathfrak{f}(x),\mathfrak{f}(y)}
satisfying:

(1)

For each
x,y\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
, the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{\mathfrak{f}(x),\mathfrak{f}(y)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(\mathfrak{f}(x),\mathfrak{f}(y))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{f}}
projection

(2)

For each
u,v\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
, the induced map

\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\\
\mathfrak{f}(x)=u,\mathfrak{f}(y)=v\end{subarray}}\iota_{x,y}:\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\\
\mathfrak{f}(x)=u,\mathfrak{f}(y)=v\end{subarray}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,y)\to E_{u,v}

is a neat embedding

(3)

For each
x,y,z\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
, the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(y,z)\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,z)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{\mathfrak{f}(y),\mathfrak{f}(z)}\times E_{\mathfrak{f}(x),\mathfrak{f}(y)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{\mathfrak{f}(x),\mathfrak{f}(z)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\circ\cdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\circ\cdot}

For our construction in Section
4.8
, we need to fit a “slightly thickened” version of the moduli spaces
\mathcal{M}(x,y)
into our “greatly thickened” moduli spaces
E_{u,v}
.

Definition 4.21
.

We can, in fact, extend the embedding
\iota_{x,y}
to an embedding

\displaystyle\overline{\iota}_{x,y}:\left[\prod_{i=|v|}^{|u|-1}(-\epsilon,\epsilon)^{d_{i}}\right]\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\to\left[\prod_{i=|v|}^{|u|-1}(-R,R)^{d_{i}}\right]\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,v),

\displaystyle(a,p)\mapsto\iota_{x,y}(p)+(a,0)

\displaystyle\quad\text{for }a\in\prod_{i=|v|}^{|u|-1}(-\epsilon,\epsilon)^{d_{i}}\text{, }p\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,y)

for some small
\epsilon>0
. By Condition
1
,
\iota(\mathcal{M}(x,y))
is transverse to the fibers
\left[\prod_{i=|v|}^{|u|-1}(-R,R)^{d_{i}}\right]\times\{p\}
. So for small enough
\epsilon
,
\overline{\iota}_{x,y}
is indeed an embedding.

4.8.
The signed cubical realization

Definition 4.22
.

Fix a cubical neat embedding
\iota
of a signed cubical flow category
(\mathscr{C},\mathfrak{f}:\Sigma^{N}\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}_{C}(n),\sigma)
relative to a tuple
\mathbf{d}=(d_{0},\ldots,d_{n-1})
. From
\mathscr{C},\iota
, we build a CW complex
||\mathscr{C}||=||\mathscr{C}||_{\iota}
satisfying:

(C-1)

The CW complex
||\mathscr{C}||
has one cell for each
x\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
. Letting
u
denote
\mathfrak{f}(x)
, this cell is given by

\mathcal{C}(x)=\prod_{i=0}^{|u|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|u|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times J\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,\overline{0}),

where
J
is the usual unit interval
[0,1]
, and

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,\overline{0})=\begin{cases}[0,1]\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,\overline{0})&if $u\neq\overline{0}$\\
\{0\}&if $u=\overline{0}$.\end{cases}

(C-2)

For any
x,y\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
with
\mathfrak{f}(x)=u>\mathfrak{f}(y)=v
, the cubical neat embedding
\iota
gives an embedding

\displaystyle\jmath_{\gamma}:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(y)\times\gamma

\displaystyle\xhookrightarrow{\tau^{\sigma(\gamma)}\times\text{Id}}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(y)\times\gamma

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathcal{C}(y)\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=0}^{|v|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|v|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times J\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,\overline{0})\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\cong\prod_{i=0}^{|v|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|u|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times J\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,\overline{0})\times\left(\prod_{i=|v|}^{|u|-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,y)\right)

\displaystyle\xhookrightarrow{\text{Id}\times\tilde{\iota}_{x,y}}\prod_{i=0}^{|v|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|u|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times J\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,\overline{0})\times\left(\prod_{i=|v|}^{|u|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(u,v)\right)

\displaystyle\cong\prod_{i=0}^{|u|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|u|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times J\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,\overline{0})\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,v)

\displaystyle\xhookrightarrow{}\prod_{i=0}^{|u|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|u|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times J\times\partial(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,\overline{0}))

\displaystyle\subset\partial\mathcal{C}(x),

where
\gamma\in\widehat{A}_{x,y}
, and
\tau:\mathcal{C}(y)\to\mathcal{C}(y)
denotes a flip
t\mapsto-t
in the
J
-factor, and
Id
on all other factors (so
\tau^{\sigma(\gamma)}=\text{Id}
if and only if
\sigma(\gamma)=0
). We call the image of this map
\mathcal{C}_{y}(x)
.

(C-3)

The attaching map for
\mathcal{C}(x)
sends the subspace
\mathcal{C}_{y}(x)\cong\mathcal{C}(y)\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,y)
of the boundary
\partial\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x)
by the projection map to
\mathcal{C}(y)
, and sends the complement of
\bigcup_{y}\mathcal{C}_{y}(x)
to the basepoint.

The
signed cubical realization

\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{C})
is defined to be the formal desuspension

\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{C}):=\Sigma^{-(N+|\mathbf{d}|+1)}||\mathscr{C}||,

where
|\mathbf{d}|
denotes
d_{0}+\ldots+d_{n-1}
. (The desuspension ensures that the gradings
\gr(x)
of objects
x\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
agree with the dimensions of the corresponding “cells” in
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{C})
.)

Remark 4.22.1
.

Our construction of
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{C})
appears similar to the cubical realization
\mathcal{X}(\mathscr{C})
from
[
LLS20
]
, Section 3.7. The difference of our definition is our introduction of the
J
-factor in the cells
\mathcal{C}(x)
. The
J
-factor’s role in the attaching maps
\partial\mathcal{C}(x)\to\mathcal{C}(y)
is characterized by the potential flips
\tau^{\sigma(W)}
, which themselves depend on the signs
\sigma(W)
. Observe that if
\sigma(W)=0
for all
W
, the
J
-factor simply acts like a suspension factor, and the signed cubical realization
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{C})
agrees with
\mathcal{X}(\mathscr{C})
.

Definition 4.23
.

On the other hand, given an unsigned cubical flow category
\mathscr{C}
, we can equip to
\mathscr{C}
the trivial sign map
\sigma:=0
as in Definition
4.8
. We can then define the signed cubical realization
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{C})
of
\mathscr{C}
, which we denote
\mathcal{X}_{\emptyset}(\mathscr{C})
. We observe, as in Remark
4.22.1
,
\mathcal{X}_{\emptyset}(\mathscr{C})
is stably equivalent to
\mathcal{X}(\mathscr{C})
. We call
\mathcal{X}_{\emptyset}(\mathscr{C})
the
unsigned cubical realization
of
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
.

Lemma 4.24
.

C_{\mathcal{M}}^{*}(\mathscr{C};\mathbb{F}_{2})
is canonically isomorphic to
C_{\cell}^{*}(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{C});\mathbb{F}_{2})
, where generators
x\in\Ob(\mathscr{C})
map to the generators
\mathcal{C}(x)
of
C_{\cell}^{*}(\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{C});\mathbb{F}_{2})
. In particular,
C_{\mathcal{M}}^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{K}(L);\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong KC^{*}(L;\mathbb{F}_{2})
for any link diagram
L
.

Proof.

The one-to-one correspondence of generators is immediate from Definitions
4.10
and
4.22
. Compatibility with the differential is left as an exercise.
∎

Remark 4.24.1
.

The construction
\mathcal{X}_{\emptyset}(\mathscr{C})
is equivalent to
\mathcal{X}(\mathscr{C})
, but the addition of the
J
-factor streamlines our computation of
\Sq^{2}
on
\mathcal{X}_{\emptyset}(\mathscr{C})
. In the signed case, the
J
factor will be essential.

4.9.
The signed cubical realization is stably equivalent to the signed realization

Definition 4.25
.

For a stable Burnside functor
(F:\underline{2}^{n}\to\mathscr{B}_{\sigma},r)
, after fixing a
k
-dimensional spatial refinement
\widetilde{F}_{k}
, we define its realization as the finite CW spectrum
|\Sigma^{r}F|=(||F||_{k},r-k)
.

Proposition 4.26

(
[
SSS20
]
)
.

Let
F:\underline{2}^{n}\to\mathscr{B}_{\sigma}
be a strictly unitary, lax
2
-functor and let
\widetilde{F}_{k}
be a
k
-dimensional spatial refinement of
F
. The realization
||F||_{k}
carries a CW complex structure whose cells except the basepoint correspond to the elements of the set
\coprod_{u\in\underline{2}^{n}}F(u)
. Further, the equivalences
\Sigma\hocolim\widetilde{F}_{k}^{+}\cong\hocolim\widetilde{F}_{k+1}^{+}
can be chosen to be cellular, so
\hocolim\widetilde{F}^{+}
inherits the structure of a CW spectrum.

Proof.

The proof generalizes without changes from
[
LLS20
]
.
∎

Theorem 4.27
.

Let
(\mathscr{C},\mathfrak{f}:\Sigma^{N}\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}_{C}(n),\sigma)
be a signed cubical flow category, and let
F:\underline{2}^{n}\to\mathscr{B}_{\sigma}
be its corresponding unitary, lax
2
-functor (Lemma
4.19
). The signed cubical realization is stably homotopy equivalent to signed realization
|\Sigma^{-N}F|=(||F||_{k},-N-k)
.

The proof, which we shall outline, generalizes from
[
LLS20
]
, Theorem 8.

Proof.

First fix a cubical neat embedding
\iota
of
\mathscr{C}
, where
\iota
is relative
\mathbf{d}=(d_{0},\ldots,d_{n-1})
, thereby defining
||\mathscr{C}||:=||\mathscr{C}||_{\iota}
. Now let
k=1+\sum_{i}d_{i}
(the
1
accounts for the extra
J
-factor).

Step 1: Build a spatial refinement
\widetilde{F}_{k}
of
F
and define
||F||_{k}
.

The cubical neat embedding
\iota
determines the cells of
||\mathscr{C}||
, which are of the following form: if
u\in\Ob(\underline{2}^{n})
and
x\in F(u)
, then

\mathcal{C}(x)=\begin{cases}\prod_{i=0}^{|u|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|u|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times J\times[0,1]\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,\overline{0})&if $u\neq\overline{0}$,\\
\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times J\times\{0\}&if $u=\overline{0}$.\end{cases}

Now for
u\in\Ob{\underline{2}^{n}}
,
x\in F(u)
, we define the box
B_{x}
associated to
x
by

B_{x}=J\times\prod_{i=0}^{|u|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|u|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}

Now we define our family of box maps using
\iota
. For a chain of non-identity morphisms
v=v^{0}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}\ldots\xrightarrow{f_{m}}v^{m}=u
in
\underline{2}^{n}
, define

\widetilde{F}^{+}_{k}(f_{m},\ldots,f_{1})=\Phi(e,F(\phi_{v,u})):[0,1]^{m-1}\times\widetilde{F}^{+}_{k}(v)\to\widetilde{F}^{+}_{k}(u),

where
e:[0,1]^{m-1}\to E(\{B_{y}\ |\ y\in F(v)\},s_{F(\phi_{v,u})})
is a family of boxes we shall construct. The construction is as follows: each
\gamma\in F(\varphi_{v,u})
corresponds to a box
B_{\gamma}=\prod_{i=0}^{|u|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|u|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}
, and
e
is a
[0,1]^{m-1}
-parameter family of embeddings

\coprod_{\gamma|s(\gamma)=y}B_{\gamma}\hookrightarrow B_{y},\qquad\forall y\in F(v)

Define the embedding
\imath_{\gamma}
by the composition

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle\times B_{\gamma}

\displaystyle\xhookrightarrow{(\Id\times\tau^{\sigma(\gamma)})}\gamma\times B_{\gamma}

\displaystyle\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}(y,x)\times B_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(y,x)\times J\times\prod_{i=0}^{|u|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|u|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}

\displaystyle\cong J\times\prod_{i=0}^{|u|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\left(\prod_{i=|u|}^{|v|-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(y,x)\right)\times\prod_{i=|v|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}

\displaystyle\xhookrightarrow{(\Id,\overline{\iota}_{x,y},\Id)}J\times\prod_{i=0}^{|u|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\left(\prod_{i=|u|}^{|v|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,u)\right)\times\prod_{i=|v|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\to}^{(\Id,\pi^{R},\Id)}}J\times\prod_{i=0}^{|u|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|u|}^{|v|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|v|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}

\displaystyle\cong J\times\prod_{i=0}^{|v|-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=|v|}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}=B_{y}

and define
e_{\gamma}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,u)\times B_{\gamma}\to B_{y}
by the composition

\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,u)\times B_{\gamma}\xhookrightarrow{(\mathfrak{f}_{\gamma}^{-1},\Id)}\gamma\times B_{\gamma}\xhookrightarrow{\imath_{\gamma}}B_{y}.

Consider the induced map

(10)

\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,u)\times\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in F(\phi_{v,u})\\
s(\gamma)=y\end{subarray}}B_{\gamma}\to B_{y}.

Since
\iota
is a cubical neat embedding (in particular, see Definition
4.20
, (
2
)), it follows that for any point
\text{pt}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,u)
, the restriction
\{\text{pt}\}\times\coprod_{\gamma\in F(\phi_{v,u})\mid s(\gamma)=y}B_{\gamma}\to B_{y}
is an inclusion of disjoint sub-boxes.

The chain
v=v^{0}>\ldots>v^{m}=u
corresponds to a subcube
[0,1]^{m-1}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,u)
in the cubical complex structure of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,u)
(see
[
LLS20
]
, Lemma 3.20). Restrict the map from (
10
) to
[0,1]^{m-1}\times\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in F(\phi_{v,u})\\
s(\gamma)=y\end{subarray}}B_{\gamma}
to obtain our family
e
of sub-boxes
\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in F(\phi_{v,u})\\
s(\gamma)=y\end{subarray}}B_{\gamma}\subset B_{y}
.

Step 2: Define a cellular map
||F||_{k}\to||\mathscr{C}||
.

For any
u\in\Ob(\underline{2}^{n})
and any
x\in F(u)
, the cell associated to
x
in
||F||_{k}
is

\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(x)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,\overline{0})\times[0,2]\times B_{x}&if $u\neq\overline{0}$,\\
\{0\}\times B_{x}&if $u=\overline{0}$.\end{cases}

\mathcal{C}(x)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,\overline{0})\times[0,1]\times B_{x}&if $u\neq\overline{0}$,\\
\{0\}\times B_{x}&if $u=\overline{0}$.\end{cases}

Map
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(x)\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x)
by the quotient map
[0,2]\to[0,2]/[1,2]\cong[0,1]
, and the identity map on all other factors. This map is degree
\pm 1
on each cell, and is thus a stable equivalence as long as our map is well-defined, that is, compatible with the attaching maps of
||F||_{k}
and
||\mathscr{C}||
.

Step 3: Prove that our cellular map is well-defined.

Suppose
p\in\partial\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(y)
,
p\in N_{v}\times B_{y}
, and
p
is glued to some point
q\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(x)
under the CW complex attaching map of
\hocolim(\widetilde{F}^{+}_{k})
. We prove that
p
, now viewed as a point in
\mathcal{C}(y)
, is glued to
q
, where now
q
is viewed as a point in
\mathcal{C}(y)
. Just as in

We first outline how
p
is glued in
\hocolim(\widetilde{F}^{+}_{k})
. Let
p=(p_{1},p_{2})
, where
p_{1}\in N_{v}
,
p_{2}\in B_{y}
, and also assume
p_{1}
lies in the cube
[0,1]^{m}
associated to the chain
v=v^{0}>\ldots>v^{m}
; we say
p_{1}
has coordinates
(p_{1,1},\ldots,p_{1,l})
. Now suppose (since
p_{1}\in N_{v}
) that
p_{1,l}=0
. Denote
\widetilde{F}_{k}(\phi_{v^{l-1},v^{l}},\ldots,\phi_{v^{1},v^{0}})
by
\psi
. The point
p
is glued to a point

((p_{1,l+1},\ldots,p_{1,m}),\psi((p_{1,1},\ldots,p_{1,l-1}),p_{2})),

which we now denote as
q
. To lighten up the notation, we introduce the terms
q_{1}=(p_{1,l+1},\ldots,p_{1,m})\in\widetilde{M}_{v^{l}}
,
q_{2}=\psi((p_{1,1},\ldots,p_{1,l-1}),p_{2})\in B_{x}
,
q^{\prime}=(p_{1,1},\ldots,p_{1,l-1})\in M_{v,v^{l}}
. We include these terms in the below equation for reference:

q=\bigl(\overbrace{(p_{1,l+1},\ldots,p_{1,m})}^{q_{1}\in\widetilde{M}_{v^{l}}},\overbrace{\psi(\underbrace{(p_{1,1},\ldots,p_{1,l-1})}_{q^{\prime}\in M_{v,v^{l}}},p_{2})}^{q_{2}\in B_{x}}\bigr).

The following equalities show that
p
and
q
(both written as points in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(y)
,
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}(x)
respectively) are identified under the gluing map in
||\mathscr{C}||
:

\displaystyle\jmath_{\gamma}\left(\mathfrak{f}_{\gamma}^{-1}(q^{\prime}),q\right)

\displaystyle=\jmath_{\gamma}\left(\mathfrak{f}_{\gamma}^{-1}(q^{\prime}),q_{1},q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(q_{1}\circ q^{\prime},\imath_{\gamma}\left(\mathfrak{f}_{\gamma}^{-1}(q^{\prime}),q_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(q_{1}\circ q^{\prime},e_{\gamma}(q^{\prime},q_{2})\right)

\displaystyle=\left((p_{1,1},\ldots,p_{1,l-1},0,p_{1,l+1},\ldots,p_{1,m}),p_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=(p_{1},p_{2})=p.

The second to last equality is justified in Figure
15
.

Figure 15.
We have
e_{\gamma}(q^{\prime},q_{2})=p_{2}
because
\psi(q^{\prime},p_{2})=q_{2}
.

∎

4.10.
Truncating the cubical realization to three adjacent dimensions

We denote the cubical realization
||\mathscr{C}||
of a flow category
\mathscr{C}
by
Y
, and we let
M=|\mathbf{d}|+1+l+N
. This choice of
M
comes from the identity

\Sigma^{-l}C^{*}(\mathcal{X}(\mathscr{C});\mathbb{F}_{2})=C_{\cell}^{*}(\Sigma^{-(|\mathbf{d}|+1+l+N)}Y;\mathbb{F}_{2})=\Sigma^{-M}C_{\cell}^{*}(Y;\mathbb{F}_{2}).

and the followng fact that
\Sq^{2}:H^{l}(\mathcal{X}(\mathscr{C});\mathbb{F}_{2})\to H^{l}(\mathcal{X}(\mathscr{C});\mathbb{F}_{2})
is precisely
\Sq^{2}:H^{M}(Y^{\prime};\mathbb{F}_{2})\to H^{M}(Y^{\prime};\mathbb{F}_{2})
. We will show that now all the cells of
Y
are relevant to us, namely that we only care about the
M
,
(M+1)
, and
(M+2)
-dimensional cells of
Y
. These cells can be repackaged into another CW complex, which is the cubical realization of a “truncated” flow category.

Definition 4.28
.

Define the
truncated
signed cubical flow category
(\mathscr{C}^{\prime},\mathfrak{f}^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
as follows:

•

\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
is the full subcategory of
\mathscr{C}
containing precisly the objects of grading
l,l+1,l+2
.

•

The grading-preserving functor
\mathfrak{f}^{\prime}:\Sigma^{N}\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)
is the restriction of
\mathfrak{f}
to
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
.

•

Similarly,
\sigma^{\prime}
is the restriction of
\sigma
to
\coprod_{x,y\in\Ob(\mathscr{C}^{\prime})}\widehat{A}_{x,y}
.

By an abuse of notation, we identify
\mathfrak{f}^{\prime}
with
\mathfrak{f}
and
\sigma^{\prime}
with
\sigma
.

We define a cubical neat embedding
\iota^{\prime}
relative to the “truncated” tuple

\mathbf{d}^{\prime}=(0,\ldots,0,d_{\kappa},d_{\kappa+1},0,\ldots,0)=:(0,\ldots,0,A,B,0,\ldots,0).

The embeddings
\iota^{\prime}_{y,x}
defined exactly the same as the embeddings
\iota_{y,x}
of
\mathscr{C}
. We define the
truncated
cubical realization of
\mathscr{C}
to be
||\mathscr{C}^{\prime}||=||\mathscr{C}^{\prime}||_{\iota}
, and we define
\mathcal{X}(\mathscr{C}^{\prime})=\Sigma^{-(N+|\mathbf{d}+1)}||\mathscr{C}^{\prime}||
. We let
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x)
denote the cell of
||\mathscr{C}^{\prime}||
corresponding to
x\in\Ob(\mathscr{C}^{\prime})
.

Objects of grading
l
(resp.
l+1
,
l+2
), we often name
x
(resp.
y
,
z
). In this spirit, the cells of
Y^{\prime}
are of the type

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=[-R,R]^{A}\times[-R,R]^{B}\times J\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(w,\overline{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\mathfrak{f}(z)=w,

\displaystyle\quad|w|=\kappa+2

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(y)

\displaystyle=[-R,R]^{A}\times[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{B}\times J\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,\overline{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\mathfrak{f}(y)=v,

\displaystyle\quad|v|=\kappa+1

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{A}\times[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{B}\times J\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,\overline{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\mathfrak{f}(x)=u,

\displaystyle\quad|u|=\kappa,

where we define
\kappa:=l+N
,
A=d_{\kappa}
,
B=d_{\kappa+1}
. The cells are
Y^{\prime}
are of three consecutive dimensions; namely, if we define
m:=A+B+1+l+N
, then
\mathcal{C}(x)
,
\mathcal{C}(y)
,
\mathcal{C}(z)
are
m
-dimensional,
m+1
-dimensional, and
m+2
-dimensional cells.

Now

also consists of cells of three consecutive dimensions; the
M
,
(M+1)
, and
(M+2)
-dimensional cells of
Y
, which we write as

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=0}^{(\kappa+2)-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=\kappa+2}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times J\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(w,\overline{0})\qquad\mathfrak{f}(z)=w,\quad|w|=\kappa+2

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(y)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=0}^{(\kappa+1)-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=\kappa+1}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times J\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(v,\overline{0})\qquad\mathfrak{f}(y)=v,\quad|v|=\kappa+1

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(x)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=0}^{\kappa-1}[-R,R]^{d_{i}}\times\prod_{i=\kappa}^{n-1}[-\epsilon,\epsilon]^{d_{i}}\times J\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathscr{C}_{C}(n)}(u,\overline{0})\qquad\mathfrak{f}(x)=u,\quad|u|=\kappa,

We observe that

Proposition 4.29
.

There is a CW structure on
\Sigma^{|\mathbf{d}|-|\mathbf{d}^{\prime}|}Y^{\prime}
, where the cells of
\Sigma^{|\mathbf{d}|-|\mathbf{d}^{\prime}|}Y^{\prime}
corresponding to
x

are copies of the cells
\mathcal{C}(x)
,
\mathcal{C}(y)
,
\mathcal{C}(z)
. In fact, identifying the cells of
\Sigma^{|\mathbf{d}|-|\mathbf{d}^{\prime}|}Y^{\prime}
with their corresponding cells
\mathcal{C}(x)
,
\mathcal{C}(y)
,
\mathcal{C}(z)
induces a homeomorphism

\Sigma^{|\mathbf{d}|-|\mathbf{d}^{\prime}|}Y^{\prime}\cong Y^{(M+2)}/Y^{(M-1)}.

Proof.

The identification of cells respects the attaching maps, because the cubical neat embedding
\iota^{\prime}
of
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
can be viewed as a “restriction” of the cubical neat embedding
\iota
. Therfore, the identification of CW complexes is well-defined.
∎

By making sure
Y:=||\mathscr{C}||
is defined witih
d_{\kappa},d_{\kappa+1}\geq 2
if necessary, we can assume that
A,B>1
.

\mathcal{C}(x)
,
\mathcal{C}(y)
,

are
m
-dimensional,

m+2

.

Procedure 4.30
.

We can compute
\Sq^{2}:H^{M}(Y;\mathbb{F}_{2})\to H^{M+2}(Y;\mathbb{F}_{2})
as follows: For any element

. This computation allows us to compute
\Sq^{2}:H^{l}(\mathcal{X}(\mathscr{C});\mathbb{F}_{2})\to H^{l+2}(\mathcal{X}(\mathscr{C});\mathbb{F}_{2})

We include a diagram comtaining the spaces we constructed for reference:

subquotient
